402: +// eventLog collects the interface events of a registry by interface name.
403: +type eventLog struct {
404: +	mu      sync.Mutex
405: +	actions map[string][]string
406: +}
407: +
408: +func collectEvents(ctx context.Context, events <-chan aggregatedEvent) *eventLog {
409: +	l := &eventLog{actions: map[string][]string{}}
410: +
411: +	go func() {
412: +		for {
413: +			select {
414: +			case ev := <-events:
415: +				l.mu.Lock()
416: +				name := ev.Attributes["ifname"]
417: +				l.actions[name] = append(l.actions[name], ev.Action)
418: +				l.mu.Unlock()
419: +			case <-ctx.Done():
420: +				return
421: +			}
422: +		}
423: +	}()
424: +
425: +	return l
426: +}
427: +
428: +func (l *eventLog) get(name string) []string {
429: +	l.mu.Lock()
430: +	defer l.mu.Unlock()
431: +
432: +	return slices.Clone(l.actions[name])
433: +}
434: +
435: +// waitFor waits until every name has an event with one of the actions.
436: +func (l *eventLog) waitFor(t *testing.T, names []string, actions ...string) {
437: +	t.Helper()
438: +
439: +	deadline := time.Now().Add(3 * time.Second)
440: +	for {
441: +		var missing []string
442: +		for _, name := range names {
443: +			if !slices.ContainsFunc(l.get(name), func(a string) bool {
444: +				return slices.Contains(actions, a)
445: +			}) {
446: +				missing = append(missing, name)
447: +			}
448: +		}
449: +
450: +		if len(missing) == 0 {
451: +			return
452: +		}
453: +
454: +		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
455: +			t.Fatalf("no %v event for %v", actions, missing)
456: +		}
457: +
458: +		time.Sleep(10 * time.Millisecond)
459: +	}
460: +}

462: +// newTestNetns creates a named network namespace without leaving the
463: +// current thread in it.
464: +func newTestNetns(t *testing.T) (string, *netlink.Handle) {
465: +	t.Helper()
466: +
467: +	if os.Geteuid() != 0 {
468: +		t.Skip("requires root to create network namespaces")
469: +	}
470: +
471: +	runtime.LockOSThread()
472: +	defer runtime.UnlockOSThread()
473: +
474: +	orig, err := netns.Get()
475: +	if err != nil {
476: +		t.Fatalf("failed to get current netns: %v", err)
477: +	}
478: +	defer orig.Close()
479: +
480: +	name := fmt.Sprintf("clabtst-ev-%d-%d", os.Getpid(), time.Now().UnixNano())
481: +
482: +	ns, err := netns.NewNamed(name)
483: +	if err != nil {
484: +		t.Fatalf("failed to create netns: %v", err)
485: +	}
486: +
487: +	if err := netns.Set(orig); err != nil {
488: +		t.Fatalf("failed to return to the original netns: %v", err)
489: +	}
490: +
491: +	handle, err := netlink.NewHandleAt(ns)
492: +	if err != nil {
493: +		t.Fatalf("failed to create netlink handle: %v", err)
494: +	}
495: +
496: +	t.Cleanup(func() {
497: +		handle.Close()
498: +		ns.Close()
499: +		_ = netns.DeleteNamed(name)
500: +	})
501: +
502: +	return "/run/netns/" + name, handle
503: +}

535: +func startRegistry(
536: +	t *testing.T,
537: +	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
538: +) (*netlinkRegistry, *eventLog) {
539: +	t.Helper()
540: +
541: +	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
542: +	events := make(chan aggregatedEvent, 1024)
543: +	registry := newNetlinkRegistry(ctx, events, true, false, 0)
544: +	collected := collectEvents(ctx, events)
545: +
546: +	registry.Start(container)
547: +	t.Cleanup(func() {
548: +		registry.Stop(container.ID)
549: +		cancel()
550: +	})
551: +
552: +	return registry, collected
553: +}

668: +func TestProcessExistingAfterResubscribe(t *testing.T) {
669: +	link := func(mtu int) netlink.Link {
670: +		return &netlink.Dummy{LinkAttrs: netlink.LinkAttrs{Index: 7, Name: "eth1", MTU: mtu}}

677: +		want            []string

681: +			initial:         true,
682: +			want:            []string{"snapshot"},
683: +		},
684: +		"first subscription without snapshot": {initial: true},
685: +		"new after resubscribe":               {want: []string{"create"}},
686: +		"changed after resubscribe":           {known: link(1400), want: []string{"update"}},
687: +		"unchanged after resubscribe":         {known: link(1500)},
688: +	}
689: +

691: +		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {

694: +			watcher := &netlinkWatcher{

696: +				includeSnapshot: tt.includeSnapshot,
697: +			}
698: +

700: +			if tt.known != nil {
701: +				states[7] = snapshotFromLink(tt.known)
702: +			}

704: +			watcher.processExisting(states, nil, link(1500), nil, registry, tt.initial)
705: +			close(events)
706: +
707: +			var got []string
708: +			for ev := range events {
709: +				got = append(got, ev.Action)
710: +			}
711: +
712: +			if !slices.Equal(got, tt.want) {
713: +				t.Fatalf("actions = %v, want %v", got, tt.want)
714: +			}
715: +
716: +			if states[7].MTU != 1500 {
717: +				t.Fatalf("state MTU = %d, want 1500", states[7].MTU)
718: +			}
719: +		})

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n32: - Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n35: \n36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.\n\n(End of file - total 36 lines)\n</content>",

Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. 53: func (r *netlinkRegistry) Start(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) {
54: 	clone := cloneContainer(container)
55: 	if clone == nil {
56: 		return
57: 	}
58: 
59: 	id := clone.ID
60: 	if id == "" {
61: 		id = clone.ShortID
62: 	}
63: 
64: 	if id == "" {
65: 		return
66: 	}
67: 
68: 	r.mu.Lock()
69: 	if _, exists := r.watchers[id]; exists {
70: 		r.mu.Unlock()
71: 
72: 		return
73: 	}
74: 
75: 	watcherCtx, cancel := context.WithCancel(r.ctx)
76: 	watcher := &netlinkWatcher{
77: 		container:       clone,
78: 		cancel:          cancel,
79: 		done:            make(chan struct{}),
80: 		includeSnapshot: r.includeInitialSnapshot,
81: 		includeStats:    r.includeStats,
82: 		statsInterval:   r.statsInterval,
83: 	}
84: 
85: 	r.watchers[id] = watcher
86: 	r.mu.Unlock()
87: 
88: 	go watcher.run(watcherCtx, r)
89: }
90: 
91: func (r *netlinkRegistry) Stop(id string) {
92: 	if id == "" {
93: 		return
94: 	}
95: 
96: 	r.mu.Lock()
97: 	watcher, ok := r.watchers[id]
98: 	if ok {
99: 		delete(r.watchers, id)
100: 	}
101: 	r.mu.Unlock()
102: 
103: 	if !ok {
104: 		return
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	watcher.cancel()
108: 	<-watcher.done
109: }

111: func (r *netlinkRegistry) remove(id string, watcher *netlinkWatcher) {
112: 	if id == "" {
113: 		return
114: 	}
115: 
116: 	r.mu.Lock()
117: 	defer r.mu.Unlock()
118: 
119: 	if current, ok := r.watchers[id]; ok && current == watcher {
120: 		delete(r.watchers, id)
121: 	}
122: }

124: func (r *netlinkRegistry) HandleContainerEvent(
125: 	runtime clabruntime.ContainerRuntime,
126: 	ev clabruntime.ContainerEvent,
127: ) {
128: 	if !strings.EqualFold(ev.Type, clabruntime.EventTypeContainer) {
129: 		return
130: 	}
131: 
132: 	action := strings.ToLower(ev.Action)
133: 
134: 	switch action {
135: 	case clabruntime.EventActionStart,
136: 		clabruntime.EventActionUnpause,
137: 		clabruntime.EventActionRestart:
138: 		container := containerFromEvent(runtime, ev)
139: 		if container != nil {
140: 			r.Start(container)
141: 		}
142: 	case clabruntime.EventActionDie,
143: 		clabruntime.EventActionStop,
144: 		clabruntime.EventActionDestroy,
145: 		clabruntime.EventActionKill:
146: 		id := ev.ActorFullID
147: 		if id == "" {
148: 			id = ev.ActorID
149: 		}
150: 
151: 		r.Stop(id)
152: 	}
153: }

155: func cloneContainer(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) *clabruntime.GenericContainer {
156: 	if container == nil {
157: 		return nil
158: 	}
159: 
160: 	clone := &clabruntime.GenericContainer{
161: 		Names:   append([]string{}, container.Names...),
162: 		ID:      container.ID,
163: 		ShortID: container.ShortID,
164: 		Labels:  cloneStringMap(container.Labels),
165: 	}
166: 
167: 	if clone.ShortID == "" {
168: 		clone.ShortID = clabutils.ShortID(clone.ID)
169: 	}
170: 
171: 	if container.Runtime == nil {
172: 		return nil
173: 	}
174: 
175: 	clone.SetRuntime(container.Runtime)
176: 
177: 	return clone
178: }

180: func containerFromEvent(
181: 	runtime clabruntime.ContainerRuntime,
182: 	ev clabruntime.ContainerEvent,
183: ) *clabruntime.GenericContainer {
184: 	attributes := ev.Attributes
185: 
186: 	name := ev.ActorName
187: 	if name == "" && attributes != nil {
188: 		name = attributes["name"]
189: 	}
190: 
191: 	id := ev.ActorFullID
192: 	if id == "" {
193: 		id = ev.ActorID
194: 	}
195: 
196: 	if id == "" && name == "" {
197: 		return nil
198: 	}
199: 
200: 	short := ev.ActorID
201: 	if short == "" {
202: 		short = id
203: 	}
204: 
205: 	container := &clabruntime.GenericContainer{
206: 		ID:      id,
207: 		ShortID: clabutils.ShortID(short),
208: 	}
209: 
210: 	if name != "" {
211: 		container.Names = []string{name}
212: 	}
213: 
214: 	if attributes != nil {
215: 		for _, key := range []string{
216: 			clabconstants.Containerlab,
217: 			clabconstants.NodeName,
218: 			clabconstants.RootNodeName,
219: 		} {
220: 			if v := attributes[key]; v != "" {
221: 				if container.Labels == nil {
222: 					container.Labels = map[string]string{}
223: 				}
224: 
225: 				container.Labels[key] = v
226: 			}
227: 		}
228: 	}
229: 
230: 	if container.ShortID == "" {
231: 		container.ShortID = clabutils.ShortID(container.ID)
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	if runtime != nil {
235: 		container.SetRuntime(runtime)
236: 	}
237: 
238: 	return container
239: }
240: 
241: type netlinkWatcher struct {
242: 	container       *clabruntime.GenericContainer
243: 	cancel          context.CancelFunc
244: 	done            chan struct{}
245: 	includeSnapshot bool
246: 	includeStats    bool
247: 	statsInterval   time.Duration
248: 	nsHandle        netns.NsHandle
249: }
250: 
251: // Retry settings for a watcher that could not subscribe to link updates.
252: var (
253: 	watchMaxAttempts   = 5
254: 	watchRetryDelay    = time.Second
255: 	watchMaxRetryDelay = 10 * time.Second
256: )
257: 
258: func (w *netlinkWatcher) run(ctx context.Context, registry *netlinkRegistry) {
259: 	defer close(w.done)
260: 	if w.container == nil {
261: 		return
262: 	}
263: 
264: 	defer registry.remove(w.container.ID, w)
265: 
266: 	containerName := firstContainerName(w.container)
267: 	if w.container.Runtime == nil {
268: 		log.Debugf("container %s has no runtime, skipping netlink watcher", containerName)
269: 
270: 		return
271: 	}
272: 
273: 	// The states outlive a subscription, so a resubscribe only reports what
274: 	// changed while no subscription was active.
275: 	states := make(map[int]ifaceSnapshot)
276: 
277: 	var statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample
278: 	if w.includeStats {
279: 		statsSamples = make(map[int]ifaceStatsSample)
280: 	}
281: 
282: 	initial := true
283: 	failures := 0
284: 	delay := watchRetryDelay
285: 
286: 	for {
287: 		subscribed, err := w.watch(ctx, registry, states, statsSamples, initial)
288: 		if ctx.Err() != nil {
289: 			return
290: 		}
291: 
292: 		if subscribed {
293: 			initial = false
294: 			failures = 0
295: 			delay = watchRetryDelay
296: 
297: 			log.Warnf(
298: 				"netlink subscription for container %s ended, resubscribing: %v",
299: 				containerName,
300: 				err,
301: 			)
302: 		} else {
303: 			failures++
304: 			if failures >= watchMaxAttempts {
305: 				log.Warnf("stopped watching interfaces of container %s: %v", containerName, err)
306: 
307: 				return
308: 			}
309: 
310: 			log.Debugf(
311: 				"failed to watch interfaces of container %s, retrying in %s: %v",
312: 				containerName,
313: 				delay,
314: 				err,
315: 			)
316: 		}
317: 
318: 		select {
319: 		case <-time.After(delay):
320: 		case <-ctx.Done():
321: 			return
322: 		}
323: 
324: 		delay = min(delay*2, watchMaxRetryDelay)
325: 	}
326: }
327: 
328: // watch subscribes to link updates in the container's namespace and handles
329: // them until ctx ends or the subscription fails. It reports whether it
330: // subscribed. The subscription lists the existing links itself, so no change
331: // between listing and subscribing is lost.
332: func (w *netlinkWatcher) watch(
333: 	ctx context.Context,
334: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
335: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
336: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
337: 	initial bool,
338: ) (bool, error) {
339: 	nsPath, err := waitForNamespacePath(ctx, w.container.Runtime, w.container.ID)
340: 	if err != nil {
341: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve netns: %w", err)
342: 	}
343: 
344: 	nsHandle, err := netns.GetFromPath(nsPath)
345: 	if err != nil {
346: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to open netns: %w", err)
347: 	}
348: 	defer nsHandle.Close()
349: 
350: 	w.nsHandle = nsHandle
351: 
352: 	netHandle, err := netlink.NewHandleAt(nsHandle)
353: 	if err != nil {
354: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to create netlink handle: %w", err)
355: 	}
356: 	defer netHandle.Close()
357: 
358: 	netemQuery := newNetemQuerier(nsHandle)
359: 	defer netemQuery.Close()
360: 
361: 	netemInfos := netemQuery.Query()
362: 
363: 	updates := make(chan netlink.LinkUpdate, 32)
364: 	done := make(chan struct{})
365: 	defer close(done)
366: 
367: 	var (
368: 		errMu     sync.Mutex
369: 		lastError error
370: 	)
371: 
372: 	opts := netlink.LinkSubscribeOptions{
373: 		Namespace:    &nsHandle,
374: 		ListExisting: true,
375: 		// An interrupted listing is reported here too. It needs no action:
376: 		// links that changed during the listing also arrive as updates.
377: 		ErrorCallback: func(err error) {
378: 			log.Debugf(
379: 				"netlink subscription for container %s: %v",
380: 				firstContainerName(w.container),
381: 				err,
382: 			)
383: 
384: 			errMu.Lock()
385: 			lastError = err
386: 			errMu.Unlock()
387: 		},
388: 	}
389: 
390: 	if err := netlink.LinkSubscribeWithOptions(updates, done, opts); err != nil {
391: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to subscribe to netlink updates: %w", err)
392: 	}
393: 
394: 	if len(states) > 0 {
395: 		w.removeVanishedLinks(netHandle, states, statsSamples, registry)
396: 	}
397: 
398: 	// Set up netem polling interval (reuse stats interval or default to 1s).
399: 	netemPollInterval := w.statsInterval
400: 	if netemPollInterval == 0 {
401: 		netemPollInterval = time.Second
402: 	}
403: 	netemTicker := time.NewTicker(netemPollInterval)
404: 	defer netemTicker.Stop()
405: 
406: 	var (
407: 		ticker  *time.Ticker
408: 		tickerC <-chan time.Time
409: 	)
410: 	if w.includeStats && w.statsInterval > 0 {
411: 		ticker = time.NewTicker(w.statsInterval)
412: 		tickerC = ticker.C
413: 		defer ticker.Stop()
414: 	}
415: 
416: 	for {
417: 		select {
418: 		case <-tickerC:
419: 			w.collectAndEmitStats(netHandle, states, statsSamples, registry)
420: 		case <-netemTicker.C:
421: 			w.pollNetemChanges(states, registry, netemQuery)
422: 		case <-ctx.Done():
423: 			return true, nil
424: 		case update, ok := <-updates:
425: 			if !ok {
426: 				errMu.Lock()
427: 				err := lastError
428: 				errMu.Unlock()
429: 
430: 				if err == nil {
431: 					err = errors.New("update channel closed")
432: 				}
433: 
434: 				return true, err
435: 			}
436: 
437: 			// Replies to the listing carry NLM_F_MULTI, notifications do not.
438: 			if update.Header.Flags&unix.NLM_F_MULTI != 0 {
439: 				w.processExisting(states, statsSamples, update.Link, netemInfos, registry, initial)
440: 
441: 				continue
442: 			}
443: 
444: 			w.processUpdate(states, statsSamples, update, registry, netemQuery)
445: 		}
446: 	}
447: }
448: 
449: // processExisting records a link listed when the subscription started. On the
450: // first subscription a new link is reported as a snapshot when requested; after
451: // a resubscribe it is reported as created.
452: func (w *netlinkWatcher) processExisting(
453: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
454: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
455: 	link netlink.Link,
456: 	netemInfos map[int]netemInfo,
457: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
458: 	initial bool,
459: ) {
460: 	if link == nil || link.Attrs() == nil {
461: 		return
462: 	}
463: 
464: 	snapshot := withNetem(snapshotFromLink(link), netemInfos)
465: 	previous, exists := states[snapshot.Index]
466: 
467: 	if exists && snapshot.equal(previous) {
468: 		return
469: 	}
470: 
471: 	states[snapshot.Index] = snapshot
472: 	if statsSamples != nil {
473: 		if sample, ok := newStatsSample(snapshot, time.Now()); ok {
474: 			statsSamples[snapshot.Index] = sample
475: 		}
476: 	}
477: 
478: 	switch {
479: 	case exists:
480: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "update", snapshot)
481: 	case !initial:
482: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "create", snapshot)
483: 	case w.includeSnapshot:
484: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "snapshot", snapshot)
485: 	}
486: }
487: 
488: // removeVanishedLinks reports the links that were deleted while no
489: // subscription was active.
490: func (w *netlinkWatcher) removeVanishedLinks(
491: 	netHandle *netlink.Handle,
492: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
493: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
494: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
495: ) {
496: 	current, err := snapshotInterfaces(netHandle)
497: 	if err != nil {
498: 		log.Debugf(
499: 			"failed to list interfaces of container %s: %v",
500: 			firstContainerName(w.container),
501: 			err,
502: 		)
503: 
504: 		return
505: 	}
506: 
507: 	for idx, snapshot := range states {
508: 		if _, ok := current[idx]; ok {
509: 			continue
510: 		}
511: 
512: 		delete(states, idx)
513: 		delete(statsSamples, idx)
514: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "delete", snapshot)
515: 	}
516: }
517: 
518: func (w *netlinkWatcher) processUpdate(
519: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
520: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
521: 	update netlink.LinkUpdate,
522: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
523: 	netemQuery *netemQuerier,
524: ) {
525: 	if update.Link == nil {
526: 		return
527: 	}
528: 
529: 	attrs := update.Link.Attrs()
530: 	if attrs == nil {
531: 		return
532: 	}
533: 
534: 	snapshot := snapshotFromLink(update.Link)
535: 	previous, exists := states[snapshot.Index]
536: 
537: 	switch update.Header.Type {
538: 	case unix.RTM_DELLINK:
539: 		if exists {
540: 			snapshot = previous
541: 		}
542: 
543: 		delete(states, snapshot.Index)
544: 		delete(statsSamples, snapshot.Index)
545: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "delete", snapshot)
546: 	case unix.RTM_NEWLINK:
547: 		// Query netem info for this interface
548: 		snapshot = withNetem(snapshot, netemQuery.Query())
549: 
550: 		if exists && snapshot.equal(previous) {
551: 			return
552: 		}
553: 
554: 		action := "create"
555: 		if exists {
556: 			action = "update"
557: 		}
558: 
559: 		states[snapshot.Index] = snapshot
560: 		if w.includeStats && statsSamples != nil {
561: 			if sample, ok := newStatsSample(snapshot, time.Now()); ok {
562: 				statsSamples[snapshot.Index] = sample
563: 			}
564: 		}
565: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, action, snapshot)
566: 	}
567: }
568: 
569: // pollNetemChanges checks for netem changes and emits update events.
570: func (w *netlinkWatcher) pollNetemChanges(
571: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
572: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
573: 	netemQuery *netemQuerier,
574: ) {
575: 	netemInfos := netemQuery.Query()
576: 
577: 	for idx, snapshot := range states {
578: 		previous := snapshot
579: 		info, hasNetem := netemInfos[idx]
580: 
581: 		if hasNetem {
582: 			snapshot.HasNetem = true
583: 			snapshot.Delay = info.Delay
584: 			snapshot.Jitter = info.Jitter
585: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = info.PacketLoss
586: 			snapshot.Rate = info.Rate
587: 			snapshot.Corruption = info.Corruption
588: 		} else if snapshot.HasNetem {
589: 			// Netem was removed
590: 			snapshot.HasNetem = false
591: 			snapshot.Delay = ""
592: 			snapshot.Jitter = ""
593: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = 0
594: 			snapshot.Rate = 0
595: 			snapshot.Corruption = 0
596: 		}
597: 
598: 		if !snapshot.equal(previous) {
599: 			states[idx] = snapshot
600: 			registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "update", snapshot)
601: 		}
602: 	}
603: }
604: 
605: func firstContainerName(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) string {
606: 	if container == nil || len(container.Names) == 0 {
607: 		return ""
608: 	}
609: 
610: 	return container.Names[0]
611: }
612: 
613: func containerLabel(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) string {
614: 	if container == nil || container.Labels == nil {
615: 		return ""
616: 	}
617: 
618: 	return container.Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
619: }
620: 
621: func interfaceAttributes(
622: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
623: 	snapshot ifaceSnapshot,
624: ) map[string]string {
625: 	attributes := map[string]string{
626: 		"ifname": snapshot.Name,
627: 		"index":  strconv.Itoa(snapshot.Index),
628: 		"mtu":    strconv.Itoa(snapshot.MTU),
629: 		"state":  snapshot.OperState,
630: 		"type":   snapshot.Type,
631: 		"origin": "netlink",
632: 	}
633: 
634: 	if snapshot.Alias != "" {
635: 		attributes["alias"] = snapshot.Alias
636: 	}
637: 
638: 	if snapshot.MAC != "" {
639: 		attributes["mac"] = snapshot.MAC
640: 	}
641: 
642: 	if lab := containerLabel(container); lab != "" {
643: 		attributes["lab"] = lab
644: 	}
645: 
646: 	if name := firstContainerName(container); name != "" {
647: 		attributes["name"] = name
648: 	}
649: 
650: 	overlayToolsInterface(container, attributes)
651: 
652: 	// Add netem attributes if present
653: 	if snapshot.HasNetem {
654: 		if snapshot.Delay != "" {
655: 			attributes["netem_delay"] = snapshot.Delay
656: 		}
657: 		if snapshot.Jitter != "" {
658: 			attributes["netem_jitter"] = snapshot.Jitter
659: 		}
660: 		if snapshot.PacketLoss != 0 {
661: 			attributes["netem_loss"] = strconv.FormatFloat(snapshot.PacketLoss, 'f', 2, 64) + "%"
662: 		}
663: 		if snapshot.Rate != 0 {
664: 			attributes["netem_rate"] = strconv.Itoa(snapshot.Rate) + "kbit"
665: 		}
666: 		if snapshot.Corruption != 0 {
667: 			attributes["netem_corruption"] = strconv.FormatFloat(
668: 				snapshot.Corruption,
669: 				'f',
670: 				2,
671: 				64,
672: 			) + "%"
673: 		}
674: 	}
675: 
676: 	return attributes
677: }
678: 
679: // overlayToolsInterface rewrites attributes to the host-side stitch interface
680: // when one exists, matching `inspect interfaces`.
681: func overlayToolsInterface(
682: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
683: 	attributes map[string]string,
684: ) {
685: 	if container == nil {
686: 		return
687: 	}
688: 
689: 	lab, node := clabcore.TopoIdentity(container.Labels)
690: 	if lab == "" || node == "" {
691: 		return
692: 	}
693: 
694: 	topoName := attributes["alias"]
695: 	if topoName == "" {
696: 		topoName = attributes["ifname"]
697: 	}
698: 
699: 	tools, ok := clablinks.ToolsInterface(lab, node, topoName)
700: 	if !ok {
701: 		return
702: 	}
703: 
704: 	snapshot := snapshotFromLink(tools)
705: 	attributes["ifname"] = snapshot.Name
706: 	attributes["alias"] = topoName
707: 	attributes["index"] = strconv.Itoa(snapshot.Index)
708: 	attributes["mtu"] = strconv.Itoa(snapshot.MTU)
709: 	attributes["state"] = snapshot.OperState
710: 	attributes["type"] = snapshot.Type
711: 
712: 	if snapshot.MAC != "" {
713: 		attributes["mac"] = snapshot.MAC
714: 	}
715: }
716: 
717: func (r *netlinkRegistry) emitInterfaceEvent(
718: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
719: 	action string,
720: 	snapshot ifaceSnapshot,
721: ) {
722: 	if container == nil {
723: 		return
724: 	}
725: 
726: 	attributes := interfaceAttributes(container, snapshot)
727: 
728: 	event := aggregatedEvent{
729: 		Timestamp:   time.Now(),
730: 		Type:        "interface",
731: 		Action:      action,
732: 		ActorID:     container.ShortID,
733: 		ActorName:   firstContainerName(container),
734: 		ActorFullID: container.ID,
735: 		Attributes:  attributes,
736: 	}
737: 
738: 	select {
739: 	case r.events <- event:
740: 	case <-r.ctx.Done():
741: 	}
742: }
743: 
744: func waitForNamespacePath(
745: 	ctx context.Context,
746: 	runtime clabruntime.ContainerRuntime,
747: 	containerID string,
748: ) (string, error) {
749: 	const (
750: 		attempts   = 5
751: 		retryDelay = 200 * time.Millisecond
752: 	)
753: 
754: 	var lastErr error
755: 
756: 	for i := 0; i < attempts; i++ {
757: 		nsPath, err := runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, containerID)
758: 		if err == nil && nsPath != "" {
759: 			return nsPath, nil
760: 		}
761: 
762: 		if err != nil {
763: 			lastErr = err
764: 		}
765: 
766: 		select {
767: 		case <-time.After(retryDelay):
768: 		case <-ctx.Done():
769: 			return "", ctx.Err()
770: 		}
771: 	}
772: 
773: 	if lastErr != nil {
774: 		return "", lastErr
775: 	}
776: 
777: 	return "", fmt.Errorf("namespace path not ready for container %s", containerID)
778: }
779: 
780: func (r *netlinkRegistry) emitInterfaceStatsEvent(
781: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
782: 	snapshot ifaceSnapshot,
783: 	metrics ifaceStatsMetrics,
784: ) {
785: 	if !r.includeStats || container == nil || !snapshot.HasStats {
786: 		return
787: 	}
788: 
789: 	attributes := interfaceAttributes(container, snapshot)
790: 	attributes["rx_bytes"] = strconv.FormatUint(metrics.RxBytes, 10)
791: 	attributes["tx_bytes"] = strconv.FormatUint(metrics.TxBytes, 10)
792: 	attributes["rx_packets"] = strconv.FormatUint(metrics.RxPackets, 10)
793: 	attributes["tx_packets"] = strconv.FormatUint(metrics.TxPackets, 10)
794: 	attributes["rx_bps"] = strconv.FormatFloat(metrics.RxBps, 'f', -1, 64)
795: 	attributes["tx_bps"] = strconv.FormatFloat(metrics.TxBps, 'f', -1, 64)
796: 	attributes["rx_pps"] = strconv.FormatFloat(metrics.RxPps, 'f', -1, 64)
797: 	attributes["tx_pps"] = strconv.FormatFloat(metrics.TxPps, 'f', -1, 64)
798: 	attributes["interval_seconds"] = strconv.FormatFloat(metrics.Interval.Seconds(), 'f', -1, 64)
799: 
800: 	event := aggregatedEvent{
801: 		Timestamp:   metrics.Timestamp,
802: 		Type:        "interface",
803: 		Action:      "stats",
804: 		ActorID:     container.ShortID,
805: 		ActorName:   firstContainerName(container),
806: 		ActorFullID: container.ID,
807: 		Attributes:  attributes,
808: 	}
809: 
810: 	select {
811: 	case r.events <- event:
812: 	case <-r.ctx.Done():
813: 	}
814: }
815: 
816: func snapshotInterfaces(netHandle *netlink.Handle) (map[int]ifaceSnapshot, error) {
817: 	if netHandle == nil {
818: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netlink handle is nil")
819: 	}
820: 
821: 	links, err := netHandle.LinkList()
822: 	if err != nil {
823: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to list links: %w", err)
824: 	}
825: 
826: 	states := make(map[int]ifaceSnapshot, len(links))
827: 	for _, link := range links {
828: 		snapshot := snapshotFromLink(link)
829: 		states[snapshot.Index] = snapshot
830: 	}
831: 
832: 	return states, nil
833: }
834: 
835: // withNetem returns the snapshot with the netem settings of its interface.
836: func withNetem(snapshot ifaceSnapshot, netemInfos map[int]netemInfo) ifaceSnapshot {
837: 	if info, ok := netemInfos[snapshot.Index]; ok {
838: 		snapshot.HasNetem = true
839: 		snapshot.Delay = info.Delay
840: 		snapshot.Jitter = info.Jitter
841: 		snapshot.PacketLoss = info.PacketLoss
842: 		snapshot.Rate = info.Rate
843: 		snapshot.Corruption = info.Corruption
844: 	}
845: 
846: 	return snapshot
847: }
848: 
849: func snapshotFromLink(link netlink.Link) ifaceSnapshot {
850: 	attrs := link.Attrs()
851: 
852: 	snapshot := ifaceSnapshot{
853: 		Type: link.Type(),
854: 	}
855: 
856: 	if attrs != nil {
857: 		snapshot.Index = attrs.Index
858: 		snapshot.Name = attrs.Name
859: 		snapshot.Alias = attrs.Alias
860: 		snapshot.MTU = attrs.MTU
861: 		if len(attrs.HardwareAddr) > 0 {
862: 			snapshot.MAC = attrs.HardwareAddr.String()
863: 		}
864: 		snapshot.OperState = attrs.OperState.String()
865: 		if stats := attrs.Statistics; stats != nil {
866: 			snapshot.HasStats = true
867: 			snapshot.RxBytes = stats.RxBytes
868: 			snapshot.TxBytes = stats.TxBytes
869: 			snapshot.RxPackets = stats.RxPackets
870: 			snapshot.TxPackets = stats.TxPackets
871: 		}
872: 	}
873: 
874: 	return snapshot
875: }
876: 
877: type ifaceSnapshot struct {
878: 	Index     int
879: 	Name      string
880: 	Alias     string
881: 	MTU       int
882: 	MAC       string
883: 	OperState string
884: 	Type      string
885: 	HasStats  bool
886: 	RxBytes   uint64
887: 	TxBytes   uint64
888: 	RxPackets uint64
889: 	TxPackets uint64
890: 	// Netem fields
891: 	HasNetem   bool
892: 	Delay      string
893: 	Jitter     string
894: 	PacketLoss float64
895: 	Rate       int
896: 	Corruption float64
897: }
898: 
899: func (s ifaceSnapshot) equal(other ifaceSnapshot) bool {
900: 	return s.Index == other.Index &&
901: 		s.Name == other.Name &&
902: 		s.Alias == other.Alias &&
903: 		s.MTU == other.MTU &&
904: 		s.MAC == other.MAC &&
905: 		s.OperState == other.OperState &&
906: 		s.Type == other.Type &&
907: 		s.HasNetem == other.HasNetem &&
908: 		s.Delay == other.Delay &&
909: 		s.Jitter == other.Jitter &&
910: 		s.PacketLoss == other.PacketLoss &&
911: 		s.Rate == other.Rate &&
912: 		s.Corruption == other.Corruption
913: }
914: 
915: type ifaceStatsSample struct {
916: 	RxBytes   uint64
917: 	TxBytes   uint64
918: 	RxPackets uint64
919: 	TxPackets uint64
920: 	Timestamp time.Time
921: }
922: 
923: type ifaceStatsMetrics struct {
924: 	RxBytes   uint64
925: 	TxBytes   uint64
926: 	RxPackets uint64
927: 	TxPackets uint64
928: 	RxBps     float64
929: 	TxBps     float64
930: 	RxPps     float64
931: 	TxPps     float64
932: 	Interval  time.Duration
933: 	Timestamp time.Time
934: }
935: 
936: func newStatsSample(snapshot ifaceSnapshot, timestamp time.Time) (ifaceStatsSample, bool) {
937: 	if !snapshot.HasStats {
938: 		return ifaceStatsSample{}, false
939: 	}
940: 
941: 	return ifaceStatsSample{
942: 		RxBytes:   snapshot.RxBytes,
943: 		TxBytes:   snapshot.TxBytes,
944: 		RxPackets: snapshot.RxPackets,
945: 		TxPackets: snapshot.TxPackets,
946: 		Timestamp: timestamp,
947: 	}, true
948: }
949: 
950: func (w *netlinkWatcher) collectAndEmitStats(
951: 	netHandle *netlink.Handle,
952: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
953: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
954: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
955: ) {
956: 	if netHandle == nil || statsSamples == nil {
957: 		return
958: 	}
959: 
960: 	now := time.Now()
961: 	// Fetch all interfaces with one netlink call per tick instead of one call
962: 	// per interface to reduce syscall overhead in large labs.
963: 	currentStates, err := snapshotInterfaces(netHandle)
964: 	if err != nil {
965: 		log.Debugf(
966: 			"failed to collect interface stats snapshot for container %s: %v",
967: 			firstContainerName(w.container),
968: 			err,
969: 		)
970: 
971: 		return
972: 	}
973: 
974: 	for idx, state := range states {
975: 		current, ok := currentStates[idx]
976: 		if !ok {
977: 			continue
978: 		}
979: 
980: 		state.Name = current.Name
981: 		state.Alias = current.Alias
982: 		state.MTU = current.MTU
983: 		state.MAC = current.MAC
984: 		state.OperState = current.OperState
985: 		state.Type = current.Type
986: 		state.HasStats = current.HasStats
987: 		state.RxBytes = current.RxBytes
988: 		state.TxBytes = current.TxBytes
989: 		state.RxPackets = current.RxPackets
990: 		state.TxPackets = current.TxPackets
991: 		states[idx] = state
992: 
993: 		if !state.HasStats {
994: 			delete(statsSamples, idx)
995: 
996: 			continue
997: 		}
998: 
999: 		prev, ok := statsSamples[idx]
1000: 		if !ok || now.Sub(prev.Timestamp) <= 0 {
1001: 			statsSamples[idx] = ifaceStatsSample{
1002: 				RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1003: 				TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1004: 				RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1005: 				TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1006: 				Timestamp: now,
1007: 			}
1008: 
1009: 			continue
1010: 		}
1011: 
1012: 		interval := now.Sub(prev.Timestamp)
1013: 		if interval <= 0 {
1014: 			statsSamples[idx] = ifaceStatsSample{
1015: 				RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1016: 				TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1017: 				RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1018: 				TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1019: 				Timestamp: now,
1020: 			}
1021: 
1022: 			continue
1023: 		}
1024: 
1025: 		rxBytesDelta := deltaCounter(prev.RxBytes, state.RxBytes)
1026: 		txBytesDelta := deltaCounter(prev.TxBytes, state.TxBytes)
1027: 		rxPacketsDelta := deltaCounter(prev.RxPackets, state.RxPackets)
1028: 		txPacketsDelta := deltaCounter(prev.TxPackets, state.TxPackets)
1029: 
1030: 		seconds := interval.Seconds()
1031: 		if seconds <= 0 {
1032: 			statsSamples[idx] = ifaceStatsSample{
1033: 				RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1034: 				TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1035: 				RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1036: 				TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1037: 				Timestamp: now,
1038: 			}
1039: 
1040: 			continue
1041: 		}
1042: 
1043: 		metrics := ifaceStatsMetrics{
1044: 			RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1045: 			TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1046: 			RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1047: 			TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1048: 			RxBps:     float64(rxBytesDelta) * 8 / seconds,
1049: 			TxBps:     float64(txBytesDelta) * 8 / seconds,
1050: 			RxPps:     float64(rxPacketsDelta) / seconds,
1051: 			TxPps:     float64(txPacketsDelta) / seconds,
1052: 			Interval:  interval,
1053: 			Timestamp: now,
1054: 		}
1055: 
1056: 		registry.emitInterfaceStatsEvent(w.container, state, metrics)
1057: 
1058: 		statsSamples[idx] = ifaceStatsSample{

1061: 			RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1062: 			TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1063: 			Timestamp: now,
1064: 		}
1065: 	}
1066: }

1068: func deltaCounter(previous, current uint64) uint64 {
1069: 	if current >= previous {
1070: 		return current - previous
1071: 	}
1072: 
1073: 	return current
1074: }

1094: 	}

1100: // Query queries the TC qdiscs in the namespace and returns a map of
1101: // interface index to netem data.
1102: func (q *netemQuerier) Query() map[int]netemInfo {
1103: 	result := make(map[int]netemInfo)
1104: 
1105: 	qdiscs, err := q.qdiscs()
1106: 	if err != nil {
1107: 		log.Debugf("failed to query tc qdiscs: %v", err)
1108: 		return result
1109: 	}
1110: 
1111: 	for idx := range qdiscs {
1112: 		qdisc := &qdiscs[idx]
1113: 		if qdisc.Attribute.Kind != "netem" || qdisc.Netem == nil {
1114: 			continue
1115: 		}
1116: 
1117: 		info := netemInfoFromQdisc(qdisc)
1118: 		if info.hasValues() {
1119: 			result[int(qdisc.Ifindex)] = info
1120: 		}
1121: 	}
1122: 
1123: 	return result
1124: }
1125: 
1126: func (q *netemQuerier) qdiscs() ([]gotc.Object, error) {
1127: 	if q == nil {
1128: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem querier is nil")
1129: 	}
1130: 
1131: 	if err := q.ensureHandle(); err != nil {
1132: 		return nil, err
1133: 	}
1134: 
1135: 	qdiscs, err := clabnetem.Impairments(q.tcHandle)
1136: 	if err == nil {
1137: 		return qdiscs, nil
1138: 	}
1139: 
1140: 	// Retry once by reopening the tc socket in case it became stale.
1141: 	q.Close()
1142: 	if errOpen := q.ensureHandle(); errOpen != nil {
1143: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
1144: 			"failed to query tc qdiscs (%v), and failed to reopen tc socket (%v)",
1145: 			err,
1146: 			errOpen,
1147: 		)
1148: 	}
1149: 
1150: 	return clabnetem.Impairments(q.tcHandle)
1151: }
1152: 
1153: func (q *netemQuerier) ensureHandle() error {
1154: 	if q == nil {
1155: 		return fmt.Errorf("netem querier is nil")
1156: 	}
1157: 
1158: 	if q.tcHandle != nil {
1159: 		return nil
1160: 	}
1161: 
1162: 	tcnl, err := clabnetem.NewTC(int(q.nsHandle))
1163: 	if err != nil {
1164: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to open tc socket for netem query: %w", err)
1165: 	}
1166: 
1167: 	q.tcHandle = tcnl
1168: 
1169: 	return nil
1170: }

1172: type netemInfo struct {
1173: 	Delay      string
1174: 	Jitter     string
1175: 	PacketLoss float64
1176: 	Rate       int
1177: 	Corruption float64
1178: }
1179: 
1180: func (n netemInfo) hasValues() bool {
1181: 	return n.Delay != "" || n.Jitter != "" || n.PacketLoss != 0 || n.Rate != 0 || n.Corruption != 0
1182: }
1183: 
1184: func netemInfoFromQdisc(qdisc *gotc.Object) netemInfo {
1185: 	var info netemInfo
1186: 
1187: 	if qdisc.Netem == nil {
1188: 		return info
1189: 	}
1190: 
1191: 	if qdisc.Netem.Latency64 != nil && *qdisc.Netem.Latency64 != 0 {
1192: 		info.Delay = (time.Duration(*qdisc.Netem.Latency64) * time.Nanosecond).String()
1193: 	}
1194: 
1195: 	if qdisc.Netem.Jitter64 != nil && *qdisc.Netem.Jitter64 != 0 {
1196: 		info.Jitter = (time.Duration(*qdisc.Netem.Jitter64) * time.Nanosecond).String()
1197: 	}
1198: 
1199: 	if qdisc.Netem.Rate != nil && qdisc.Netem.Rate.Rate != 0 {
1200: 		info.Rate = int(qdisc.Netem.Rate.Rate * 8 / msPerSec)
1201: 	}
1202: 
1203: 	if qdisc.Netem.Corrupt != nil && qdisc.Netem.Corrupt.Probability != 0 {
1204: 		info.Corruption = math.Round((float64(qdisc.Netem.Corrupt.Probability)/
1205: 			float64(math.MaxUint32)*100)*100) / 100
1206: 	}
1207: 
1208: 	if qdisc.Netem.Qopt.Loss != 0 {
1209: 		info.PacketLoss = math.Round(
1210: 			(float64(qdisc.Netem.Qopt.Loss)/float64(math.MaxUint32)*100)*100) / 100
1211: 	}
1212: 
1213: 	return info
1214: }

It reports whether it\n// subscribed. The subscription lists the existing links itself, so no change\n// between listing and subscribing is lost.\nfunc (w *netlinkWatcher) watch(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\tregistry *netlinkRegistry,\n\tstates map[int]ifaceSnapshot,\n\tstatsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,\n\tinitial bool,\n) (bool, error) {\n\tnsPath, err := waitForNamespacePath(ctx, w.container.Runtime, w.container.ID)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to resolve netns: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tnsHandle, err := netns.GetFromPath(nsPath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to open netns: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer nsHandle.Close()\n\n\tw.nsHandle = nsHandle\n\n\tnetHandle, err := netlink.NewHandleAt(nsHandle)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to create netlink handle: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer netHandle.Close()\n\n\tnetemQuery := newNetemQuerier(nsHandle)\n\tdefer netemQuery.Close()\n\n\tnetemInfos := netemQuery.Query()\n\n\tupdates := make(chan netlink.LinkUpdate, 32)\n\tdone := make(chan struct{})\n\tdefer close(done)\n\n\tvar (\n\t\terrMu     sync.Mutex\n\t\tlastError error\n\t)\n\n\topts := netlink.LinkSubscribeOptions{\n\t\tNamespace:    &nsHandle,\n\t\tListExisting: true,\n\t\t// An interrupted listing is reported here too. On the\n// first subscription a new link is reported as a snapshot when requested; after\n// a resubscribe it is reported as created.\nfunc (w *netlinkWatcher) processExisting(\n\tstates map[int]ifaceSnapshot,\n\tstatsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,\n\tlink netlink.Link,\n\tnetemInfos map[int]netemInfo,\n\tregistry *netlinkRegistry,\n\tinitial bool,\n) {\n\tif link == nil || subscription was active.\nfunc (w *netlinkWatcher) removeVanishedLinks(\n\tnetHandle *netlink.Handle,\n\tstates map[int]ifaceSnapshot,\n\tstatsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,\n\tregistry *netlinkRegistry,\n) {\n\tcurrent, err := snapshotInterfaces(netHandle)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Debugf(\n\t\t\t\"failed to list interfaces of container %s: 290: 	states, err := snapshotInterfaces(netHandle)
291: 	if err != nil {
292: 		log.Debugf("failed to snapshot interfaces for container %s: %v", containerName, err)
293: 		states = make(map[int]ifaceSnapshot)
294: 	}
295: 
296: 	netemQuery := newNetemQuerier(nsHandle)
297: 	defer netemQuery.Close()
298: 
299: 	// Query and apply netem info to snapshots
300: 	netemInfos := netemQuery.Query()
301: 	applyNetemToSnapshots(states, netemInfos)
302: 
303: 	var statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample
304: 	if w.includeStats {
305: 		statsSamples = make(map[int]ifaceStatsSample, len(states))
306: 		now := time.Now()
307: 		for idx, snapshot := range states {
308: 			if sample, ok := newStatsSample(snapshot, now); ok {
309: 				statsSamples[idx] = sample
310: 			}
311: 		}
312: 	}
313: 
314: 	if w.includeSnapshot {
315: 		for _, snapshot := range states {
316: 			registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "snapshot", snapshot)
317: 		}
318: 	}
319: 
320: 	updates := make(chan netlink.LinkUpdate, 32)
321: 	done := make(chan struct{})
322: 	opts := netlink.LinkSubscribeOptions{Namespace: &nsHandle}
323: 
324: 	if err := netlink.LinkSubscribeWithOptions(updates, done, opts); err != nil {
325: 		log.Debugf(
326: 			"failed to subscribe to netlink updates for container %s: %v",
327: 			containerName,
328: 			err,
329: 		)
330: 
331: 		return
332: 	}
333: 
334: 	// Set up netem polling interval (reuse stats interval or default to 1s).
335: 	netemPollInterval := w.statsInterval
336: 	if netemPollInterval == 0 {
337: 		netemPollInterval = time.Second
338: 	}
339: 	netemTicker := time.NewTicker(netemPollInterval)
340: 	defer netemTicker.Stop()
341: 
342: 	var (
343: 		ticker  *time.Ticker
344: 		tickerC <-chan time.Time
345: 	)
346: 	if w.includeStats && w.statsInterval > 0 {
347: 		ticker = time.NewTicker(w.statsInterval)
348: 		tickerC = ticker.C
349: 		defer ticker.Stop()
350: 	}
351: 
352: 	for {
353: 		select {
354: 		case <-tickerC:
355: 			w.collectAndEmitStats(netHandle, states, statsSamples, registry)
356: 		case <-netemTicker.C:
357: 			w.pollNetemChanges(states, registry, netemQuery)
358: 		case <-ctx.Done():
359: 			close(done)
360: 
361: 			return
362: 		case update, ok := <-updates:
363: 			if !ok {
364: 				return
365: 			}
366: 
367: 			w.processUpdate(states, statsSamples, update, registry, netemQuery)
368: 		}
369: 	}
370: }
371: 
372: func (w *netlinkWatcher) processUpdate(
373: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
374: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
375: 	update netlink.LinkUpdate,
376: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
377: 	netemQuery *netemQuerier,
378: ) {
379: 	if update.Link == nil {
380: 		return
381: 	}
382: 
383: 	attrs := update.Link.Attrs()
384: 	if attrs == nil {
385: 		return
386: 	}
387: 
388: 	snapshot := snapshotFromLink(update.Link)
389: 	previous, exists := states[snapshot.Index]
390: 
391: 	switch update.Header.Type {
392: 	case unix.RTM_DELLINK:
393: 		if exists {
394: 			snapshot = previous
395: 		}
396: 
397: 		delete(states, snapshot.Index)
398: 		delete(statsSamples, snapshot.Index)
399: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "delete", snapshot)
400: 	case unix.RTM_NEWLINK:
401: 		// Query netem info for this interface
402: 		netemInfos := netemQuery.Query()
403: 		if info, ok := netemInfos[snapshot.Index]; ok {
404: 			snapshot.HasNetem = true
405: 			snapshot.Delay = info.Delay
406: 			snapshot.Jitter = info.Jitter
407: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = info.PacketLoss
408: 			snapshot.Rate = info.Rate
409: 			snapshot.Corruption = info.Corruption
410: 		}
411: 
412: 		if exists && snapshot.equal(previous) {
413: 			return
414: 		}
415: 
416: 		action := "create"
417: 		if exists {
418: 			action = "update"
419: 		}
420: 
421: 		states[snapshot.Index] = snapshot
422: 		if w.includeStats && statsSamples != nil {
423: 			if sample, ok := newStatsSample(snapshot, time.Now()); ok {
424: 				statsSamples[snapshot.Index] = sample
425: 			}
426: 		}
427: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, action, snapshot)
428: 	}
429: }
430: 
431: // pollNetemChanges checks for netem changes and emits update events.
432: func (w *netlinkWatcher) pollNetemChanges(
433: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
434: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
435: 	netemQuery *netemQuerier,
436: ) {
437: 	netemInfos := netemQuery.Query()
438: 
439: 	for idx, snapshot := range states {
440: 		previous := snapshot
441: 		info, hasNetem := netemInfos[idx]
442: 
443: 		if hasNetem {
444: 			snapshot.HasNetem = true
445: 			snapshot.Delay = info.Delay
446: 			snapshot.Jitter = info.Jitter
447: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = info.PacketLoss
448: 			snapshot.Rate = info.Rate
449: 			snapshot.Corruption = info.Corruption
450: 		} else if snapshot.HasNetem {
451: 			// Netem was removed
452: 			snapshot.HasNetem = false
453: 			snapshot.Delay = ""
454: 			snapshot.Jitter = ""
455: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = 0
456: 			snapshot.Rate = 0
457: 			snapshot.Corruption = 0
458: 		}
459: 
460: 		if !snapshot.equal(previous) {
461: 			states[idx] = snapshot
462: 			registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "update", snapshot)
463: 		}
464: 	}
465: }
466: 
467: func firstContainerName(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) string {
468: 	if container == nil || len(container.Names) == 0 {
469: 		return ""
470: 	}
471: 
472: 	return container.Names[0]
473: }
474: 
475: func containerLabel(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) string {
476: 	if container == nil || container.Labels == nil {
477: 		return ""
478: 	}
479: 
480: 	return container.Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
481: }
482: 
483: func interfaceAttributes(
484: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
485: 	snapshot ifaceSnapshot,
486: ) map[string]string {
487: 	attributes := map[string]string{
488: 		"ifname": snapshot.Name,
489: 		"index":  strconv.Itoa(snapshot.Index),
490: 		"mtu":    strconv.Itoa(snapshot.MTU),
491: 		"state":  snapshot.OperState,
492: 		"type":   snapshot.Type,
493: 		"origin": "netlink",
494: 	}
495: 
496: 	if snapshot.Alias != "" {
497: 		attributes["alias"] = snapshot.Alias
498: 	}
499: 
500: 	if snapshot.MAC != "" {
501: 		attributes["mac"] = snapshot.MAC
502: 	}
503: 
504: 	if lab := containerLabel(container); lab != "" {
505: 		attributes["lab"] = lab
506: 	}
507: 
508: 	if name := firstContainerName(container); name != "" {
509: 		attributes["name"] = name
510: 	}
511: 
512: 	overlayToolsInterface(container, attributes)
513: 
514: 	// Add netem attributes if present
515: 	if snapshot.HasNetem {
516: 		if snapshot.Delay != "" {
517: 			attributes["netem_delay"] = snapshot.Delay
518: 		}
519: 		if snapshot.Jitter != "" {
520: 			attributes["netem_jitter"] = snapshot.Jitter
521: 		}
522: 		if snapshot.PacketLoss != 0 {
523: 			attributes["netem_loss"] = strconv.FormatFloat(snapshot.PacketLoss, 'f', 2, 64) + "%"
524: 		}
525: 		if snapshot.Rate != 0 {
526: 			attributes["netem_rate"] = strconv.Itoa(snapshot.Rate) + "kbit"
527: 		}
528: 		if snapshot.Corruption != 0 {
529: 			attributes["netem_corruption"] = strconv.FormatFloat(
530: 				snapshot.Corruption,
531: 				'f',
532: 				2,
533: 				64,
534: 			) + "%"
535: 		}
536: 	}
537: 
538: 	return attributes
539: }

Reasoning
Now I understand the base. Key changes:

1. `run` now retries subscription failures with backoff, previously a single attempt and give up.
2. States/statsSamples maps now persist across resubscriptions (created in `run` and passed to `watch`).
3. `watch` uses `ListExisting: true` in LinkSubscribeWithOptions and processes listing replies (NLM_F_MULTI flag) via `processExisting`.
4. `removeVanishedLinks` reports links deleted while no subscription active.
5. `withNetem` replaces `applyNetemToSnapshots`.
6. ErrorCallback captures last error for reporting when channel closes.

Now potential issues:

**Issue A: `processExisting` uses stale `netemInfos`.** In `watch`, `netemInfos := netemQuery.Query()` is captured once before subscription. The listing replies arrive asynchronously over time; but netemInfos is a snapshot from before the subscribe. That's roughly the same as before (base applied netem to initial snapshot before subscription). Fine-ish. Actually base applied netem to initial snapshot. Same semantics. Not a big issue.

**Issue B: `NLM_F_MULTI` heuristic.** The code treats any update with NLM_F_MULTI flag as a "reply to the listing" and calls `processExisting` with `initial` flag. But actually, the netlink protocol: responses to a DUMP request (NLM_F_DUMP = NLM_F_ROOT|NLM_F_MATCH, with NLM_F_REQUEST) carry NLM_F_MULTI. However, could a link change notification carry NLM_F_MULTI? Typically no — async notifications (RTM_NEWLINK from kernel) don't have NLM_F_MULTI. But also... there's a subtlety: the subscription with ListExisting dumps all links, and those dump replies come with NLM_F_MULTI. But what about the initial listing when `initial` is true? The test `TestNetlinkWatcherReportsLinksAddedWhileStarting` covers races. However, there's a subtle correctness concern: `processExisting` is called with the *stale* `initial` value... Actually `initial` is a parameter passed once per `watch` invocation, correct.

But there's a deeper issue: `processExisting` is called for ANY update with NLM_F_MULTI — including replies to other dump requests? Only the listing dump is done here, so fine.

Another subtle issue: In `processExisting`, when the link exists in states and is unchanged, it returns without emitting. But on first subscription with `includeSnapshot=false`, unchanged links get no events — same as base (base emitted snapshots only if includeSnapshot). OK.

Wait — on first subscription with includeSnapshot=false, base emitted nothing for initial snapshot. New code: processExisting with initial=true and includeSnapshot=false → no emit. Good.

**Issue C: `removeVanishedLinks` called before `netemInfos` is used?** Fine.

**Issue D: stats samples initialization on first subscription.** In base, statsSamples were initialized from the initial snapshot. In head, `statsSamples` starts empty; samples are populated in `processExisting` (when a link is first recorded) and `processUpdate`. So stats sampling starts when each interface is first seen. That's fine, arguably better.

**Issue E: A potential deadlock / goroutine leak with `updates` channel buffered 32.** The subscription goroutine (internal to netlink) sends updates to the buffered channel; if the watcher loop is busy or returns, the internal goroutine could block. `done` channel closes on defer to stop the subscription goroutine. In base, `done` was closed on ctx.Done via `close(done)`. In head, `defer close(done)` covers all return paths. But there's a subtle problem: the netlink subscription goroutine may be blocked sending to `updates` (cap 32) when `watch` returns; it will remain blocked forever because nothing drains `updates` after return? Actually the netlink subscription implementation: it has an internal goroutine that reads from the socket and sends to updates channel OR checks done channel. Let me recall the netlink implementation:

```go
func (h *Handle) sockDiag(...)...
```

Actually `LinkSubscribeWithOptions` starts a goroutine that does `for { select { case <-done: return; default: ... recv ... ; select { case ch <- msg: case <-done: return } } }`. I believe it does select on done when sending. Let me check: in vishvananda/netlink subscribe.go:

```go
func (h *Handle) LinkSubscribeWithOptions(ch chan<- LinkUpdate, done chan struct{}, options LinkSubscribeOptions) error {
	...
	s.sock.Receive...
	go func() {
		defer close(ch)
		for {
			msgs, from, err := s.sock.Receive()
			...

				...

Actually there's a known implementation detail: `req` also has `SeqManager` and `ListExisting` triggers a dump. Yes, the sender goroutine selects on done. So closing done unblocks. OK.

**Issue F: `removeVanishedLinks` race.** It lists current links after subscribe (with ListExisting, listing replies flow through the updates channel). It calls `snapshotInterfaces(netHandle)` — a separate dump — concurrently with the subscription's dump. The listing replies are processed asynchronously in the select loop. So there's a race: a link deleted between `removeVanishedLinks`' listing and the subscription listing could be:
- Present in subscription listing (arrives as update later) → processExisting records it again, but it no longer exists... and no delete event ever arrives since deletion happened before the subscription was established. Hmm, but actually if the deletion happened before the kernel processed the dump request, the dump wouldn't include it. If after, then the deletion notification (RTM_DELLINK) arrives as update and processUpdate emits delete. So the sequence: removeVanishedLinks sees link in `current` (still exists at that instant), then link is deleted, subscription established... wait order: LinkSubscribeWithOptions is called BEFORE removeVanishedLinks. So the subscription exists. Any deletion after subscribe generates RTM_DELLINK notification → processed. Any deletion before subscribe: the link is in `states` (from previous subscription) but not in `current` listing → emitted as delete. But there's a race: removeVanishedLinks lists links; the listing is done via netHandle.LinkList() which is a dump request; between subscribe and this dump, deletions arrive as notifications. Those are already handled by processUpdate. The race window: a link deleted concurrently with the removeVanishedLinks dump — the dump might still include it (deleted after dump response generated), but the deletion notification also arrives after subscription → processed as update → delete event. Fine. So delete events are not lost.

**Issue G: duplicate delete events?** If link deleted while no subscription and removeVanishedLinks emits delete, then during the new subscription's listing the link is not listed (already gone). Fine.

**Issue H: In `run`, when the subscription ends normally (e.g., `err` non-nil from ErrorCallback), the code logs a `Warnf` "resubscribing" — for each container each time. Could spam logs if a subscription repeatedly fails after being established. That's minor.

**Issue O: retry loop — when `subscribed` is true and err != nil (subscription ended with error), it logs Warnf and immediately resubscribes with no delay. If the subscription dies repeatedly in a tight loop (e.g., netns disappears — netns.GetFromPath fails → subscribed=false path with delay; or channel closed immediately due to socket error), the subscribed path loops with NO delay: `watch` returns immediately, `subscribed=true`, no sleep, loop again. Could that spin hot? If LinkSubscribeWithOptions succeeds but updates channel closes immediately with an error each time — e.g., the namespace dies while the handle is valid... The subscribe would fail (return false) typically. But consider: subscription established, then the container's netns is deleted → the receive returns an error → channel closes → watch returns (true, err) → resubscribe: waitForNamespacePath fails (runtime returns error) → subscribed=false → delay path. Rare. But no backoff on the subscribed-but-failed path is a real risk of tight-loop CPU burn with log spam (Warnf each iteration). I'd flag as P3: add a small delay on the resubscribe path too.

But using a mutex anyway is fine. Actually is there a happens-before between ErrorCallback's last write and the `!ok` receive? **Now — the big one I should scrutinize: the `NLM_F_MULTI` heuristic vs. actual netlink semantics.** Let me think again about the kernel behavior for RTM_NEWLINK notifications. Kernel-generated async notifications (e.g., from link state change) use `nlmsg_notify` with flags 0 — no NLM_F_MULTI. Replies to GETLINK dumps use `nlmsg_put` with NLM_F_MULTI. So classification is sound. However — is there a case where the subscription receives the dump replies but ALSO `LinkSubscribeWithOptions` without ListExisting receives multicast messages with flags set by whoever sent it? Multicast groups receive notifications. But note: the subscription socket is bound to RTNLGRP_LINK multicast; a request made by THIS socket (the dump) gets replies. Fine.

One more: `netlink.LinkSubscribeWithOptions` with Namespace option — it opens socket in that namespace; dump replies arrive with NLM_F_MULTI. OK.

**Issue AA: `processExisting` doesn't include stats-sample logic for `w.includeStats && statsSamples != nil` but directly `if statsSamples != nil` — equivalent since statsSamples nil when not includeStats. Fine (actually slightly simpler than processUpdate which double-checks; inconsistent but harmless).

**Issue BB: In the head, the initial snapshot is now emitted per-link as listing replies arrive, asynchronously; in base, snapshots were emitted before subscription. Minor behavior change—fine.

**Issue CC: The `watch` function returns `(true, nil)` on ctx.Done — run then checks ctx.Err() and returns. Fine.

**Issue DD: Double delete events?** Consider resubscribe where a link was deleted during the gap. removeVanishedLinks emits delete and removes from states. Fine, single event.

But consider: link deleted DURING the subscription (RTM_DELLINK processed, delete event, removed from states). Subscription dies. Resubscribe: listing doesn't include it; removeVanishedLinks sees it's not in states. Fine.

**Issue EE: `states` map access concurrency:** states is only accessed in the watcher goroutine (run/watch) — run creates it, passes to watch; watch uses it; removeVanishedLinks etc. All in one goroutine. Fine.

**Issue FF: `TestNetlinkWatcherReportsLinksAddedWhileStarting` — `wg.Go` requires Go 1.25. Check go.mod.

Let me check go.mod for the Go version and whether `min` builtin is fine (Go 1.21+).

**Issue GG: The warn log on successful-subscription-end is `Warnf` even when the subscription ends because... only error paths return true with err. If channel closes with nil lastError → err = "update channel closed". OK.

**Issue HH: Potential repeated warn spam:** If subscription ends with error immediately after establishing, repeatedly — infinite hot loop with Warnf. As analyzed, requires GetNSPath to succeed while subscribe dies instantly. Possible scenario: the container netns is torn down — GetFromPath fails → false path. Another scenario: netlink socket creation succeeds but the namespace's netlink is somehow broken. Rare. Still, the absence of any delay/backoff on the resubscribe path is a genuine robustness gap; flag P3.

Actually wait, is there a scenario where subscribe succeeds but the dump fails, causing channel close? With ListExisting, the dump request is sent; if the socket errors during dump (e.g., namespace torn down mid-dump), ErrorCallback gets the error and channel closes → (true, err) → immediate resubscribe → GetNSPath probably still succeeds briefly (the netns path /proc/pid/ns/net may still exist), subscribe again... loop until the container fully dies. Each iteration is fast. Meanwhile `waitForNamespacePath` succeeds with cached nsPath. This could spin. But how fast? Each iteration does waitForNamespacePath (immediate success), GetFromPath, NewHandleAt, subscribe, dump fails. Could be many iterations/sec. With log.Warnf each iteration — log spam. I'd flag P3 with suggestion to apply the same backoff (reset only after sustained success).

Hmm, but is it realistic that the error callback error and channel close happen? When namespace disappears, the socket receives... the socket is already open; it may just not receive anything, or receive errors. Also `GetFromPath` would fail once /proc entry gone. The spin window is narrow. P3 is fair.

**Issue II: `processExisting` — the equality check `exists && snapshot.equal(previous)` — if unchanged, returns. But if unchanged and statsSamples exists... no sample update needed. Fine.

**Issue JJ: docs.** This is internal behavior (events command reliability). Is there documentation about events/watcher behavior? Maybe docs about `clab events`. Probably no doc changes needed. Let me check if any docs describe "netlink watcher" behavior or events semantics. The change alters user-visible behavior: previously, if the watcher failed to resolve netns, it gave up silently (Debugf). Now it retries and eventually warns "stopped watching interfaces of container X". Also resubscribes after subscription end. This is a bugfix; docs may not need updates. Check docs for events.

**Now let me check `netlink_test.go` in head and base to see if existing tests still pass — e.g., tests referencing `applyNetemToSnapshots` or `run` behavior.** The diff removed `applyNetemToSnapshots`. Let me grep for usages.

**Also check `types.go`, `stream.go`, `options.go` for registry-related bits and whether `nsHandle` field is used elsewhere.**

Let me also check the manifest for what's included.
